The fact is, however, that women suffer from Long Covid twice as often as men, and even in mental health, women have suffered worse. Due to the structural added burden – keyword increased care and care work – women suffer more often than men suffer from depression, sleep problems and anxiety. What's good medicine? – Good medicine is the one that treats everyone equally well. That is often not the case. As a doctor, I do not see the gender differences in daily medical life as strong as they seem to be. It is already bizarre that the majority of patients are female, the majority of the health professionals are women, the majority of medical students and also the majority of young doctors are female, and women are often not treated equally well: It starts at blood pressure cores and stops in surgery. Men were and are also the standard in studies and in the development of medical products. An example from the field of heart surgery: Art hearts are in themselves developed for men; Standard art hearts are often too big for petite women, but there is only one model. Or: Until recently, there were only unsextables. That is, medications are mainly tested on men, but a 50 kilograms heavy woman receives the drug at the same dose as a possibly 110 kilograms heavy man. However, men and women can be seen differently in medicine. They differ in muscle mass, body weight, water content. Women distribute and destroy medications in the body differently. There are also differences in the chromosomes, on the organ level, on the hormonal level, on the metabolic level and also on the social psychological level, as we have already heard. Not only the scalpel, but also the empathy is an important instrument in medicine, the conversation, communication often plays a key role in the diagnosis. The female sex is often far ahead, when it comes to taking time, listening, taking seriously. Ladies and gentlemen! Gender-specific medicine is the future. Good medicine is the one in which everyone is treated equally well. The Women's Health Report helps in this way. Thank you for this unanimous request.

Our proposal – and we will also make a request today – is to quickly increase tariff levels retroactively by 1.1.2022 in a first step. Let's start at the lowest level: In Austria, taxes start to be paid from an annual income of 11 000 euros. This is not a great deal now, and so to speak, the tax goes into the first stage. This threshold of EUR 11 000 has not been adjusted since 2009. This means that people who have so to speak always in this area of income have paid inflation-adjusted taxes at once and have lost real money because this level has not been adjusted. Our proposal is to raise this level to EUR 13 400, which means that the first EUR 1 460 per month will not have to be taxed. That's 450 euros more a year that keep people alive. All other tariff levels should also be adjusted accordingly. To make this so to speak plausible: According to our model, those who earn 1 526 euros gross per month will earn 384 euros more per year, at 2 034 euros gross, there are 412 euros more per year – by the way, this is just under the median income, that is, 50 percent earn more, 50 percent earn less in Austria – and at 2 543 euros gross it is 602 euros per year more on the wallet. Look, that's perhaps a new computer, a new laptop, that's perhaps a new couch, that's perhaps a TV, that's perhaps a short vacation, that's perhaps a necessary babysitting you need, because – also again – the ÖVP has blocked for years that there is a comprehensive extension of childcare. These are all measures that would really help people to dispel these price explosions, which all feel. But we also demand automation, because of course it is also a matter of saying: This must not be unique now – otherwise we will wait again until inflation is high enough, we will have a hard political debate again, we will have to build pressure again, you will be happy again about a basket money, it is, as it were, the same discussion year after year. Now there are different models, and you can certainly talk about whether this is a full automation or a partial automation, whether this is, so to speak, an opt-out or the like, you can discuss about it. There is a Swiss model, there is a Swedish model. It's all got something for itself, and it's like this: Work with it! However, I am surprised that you have not already worked in your house, because in 2018 – I think, 2018, yes – Parliament's budget service – I would like to say that in this area, thank you very much for your support – we asked for the impact of the abolition of the cold progression, especially on the budget – and you will be interested to examine this, Mr Finanzminister, along the different models. In general, we have had the problem that in this emergency operation, which the Federal Government has created over the past two years, we have built parallel structures with the vaccination roads, with the test roads, with additional treatment containers, to which the ideas have already been spirited. However, all these parallel structures have committed key personnel from the medical field. It was the diploma caregiver from the Altenheim, who was standing on the test road. It has been the hospital doctor who would have been needed at his internal station, but who earned more in the vaccination street. You can't blame the professionals for going where they're best paid. But if you still have to force it all, provide for a corresponding classification and maintain this parallel structure, even though there is no need at all, even though the existing cases can already be brought back into the control system for a long time, then you will worsen the conditions and working conditions of the remaining staff in the clinics and in the care facilities. We also need to act urgently with the health authorities, Mr. Federal Minister. We still have a number of districts in Austria, which do not even have an official's office, let alone sufficient health care personnel in the authorities to fulfil their administrative obligations as a health authority, to carry out the controls independently and not always to use the security forces, the police or even the federal army for contact tracking or for checking apartheids and for the like. This is the health authority's task, not the security forces or the federal army. Finally, health education, the education of the citizens, the people in Austria, so that they can deal with this viral disease in their own responsibility. Meanwhile, the virus has become endemic. This is something the experts hoped a year and a half ago that this condition would occur that this virus would be far weakened and so large a part of the population has already met that there is hardly any more to be expected to have a natural immunity in the population. Although this is not a sterile immunity, it is not a complete protection from an infection, but it is a certain protection from serious diseases. One has seen from the Omikron wave that there have been only very, very few severe diseases and hospitalizations compared to the number of infections. There is a need to inform people that, even if there is no legal obligation, they take protective measures according to their personal risk. The State should support it in providing the State with free protective equipment, including protective vaccinations and medicines, in the event of a pandemic. First, however, we need the education, the understanding of the people, which measures are actually suitable for them so that they also carry them voluntarily. A compulsion, as it is still being used as a damocles sword above the heads of the human beings, is counterproductive for any kind of Enlightenment, because pressure produces counter-pressure, and thus you reach exactly the opposite of what you have proclaimed. You see, there's a lot to do. I am aware that you are aware of many of the points I have raised. By autumn you have a few months to give your best possible, and I hope for you, but especially for the whole people in our beautiful country, that you will take these necessary preparatory measures in a timely manner so that we can no longer face these strong, restrictive measures of the past and we can save ourselves them in the future. Thank you.

Why has mortality – thank God – dropped so much? – That has two reasons: One is the omicron variant, in which the virus is more likely to be located in the upper respiratory tracts. The variant is therefore very infectious, but less on the lungs and is therefore less dangerous. Much more important, however, is that we now have a broad basic immunity in the population through vaccination and recovery. – This is the current situation, but of course we have to look ahead. If we are dealing today – point two – with the decision whether or not to extend the COVID-19 Act, we need to know what it means if we do not extend it. That would mean that it would end in the middle of the year – it could perhaps be extended by the government until the end of this year, but would certainly not be over the winter, over the cold season. Today, therefore, the decision to amend the COVID-19 Act by mid-2023, with a possibility of extension by the end of 2023. But that does not mean – that is, point three – that now, immediately or beyond this time, several measures will be put in place until the next year, but that this law only creates a framework. This makes it possible to respond in a timely and forward-looking manner if necessary. In this law is in § 1 para. 7 clearly defined the conditions that require action: Transmissibility, infection, severity of disease, burden of health care, existing medicines, effect of vaccination. – All of this has to be taken into account, only on the basis of these factors can any further restrictive measures be put in place. All measures that would intervene in personal freedom are also subject to democratic legitimacy, as they must be confirmed by the National Council's main committee. So this draft is a framework, and we are glad that we have this framework for the autumn – because it would simply be easy not to have it! [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] What can the future look like? – We now know from this virus that it does not adhere to political requirements and even limited scientific findings, says: it changes and you have to adapt. We don't know what's next. It is always said that in autumn there will be a wave again, probably a new variant, – we do not know! New variant: can be, yes, in autumn, in a year or next week – or not, we don't know. We now have a broad basic immunity, and that is why the virus – whatever it comes – will find a different situation. This makes me cautiously optimistic. I conclude with the hope that we will hopefully need as few as possible of the possibilities offered by this framework law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-04-27-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00153_d7e24078	Madam President. Estimated Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! Worm.]] But now on the matter: What is it about the EU vocational recognition law health profession 2022? – Changes are made in eight areas, namely in the Medical Law, the Pharmacy Act, the Pharmacy Chamber Act, the Wage Cass Law, the midwives Act, the Veterinary Law, the Dentist Law and the Dentist Chamber Law. All of this sounds very complicated, but it's relatively simple: The purpose of this legislative amendment is to create an EU-compliant legal situation regarding the recognition of professional qualifications from other EU Member States, EEA Contracting States and Switzerland in the health professions concerned. In the run-up to the 2013 amendment to the EU Directive, there was a divergent legal opinion between the European Commission and Austria. Therefore, there was also a infringement procedure in which Austria took the broad view that it was based on the nature of the infringement. 4f para. 6 the non-application of the provision for sectoral occupations results in no national transposition obligation of partial access to employment for these occupations. However, the ECJ decided in 2021 and confirmed the legal view of the European Commission, so that a corresponding implementation obligation for partial access to the professions of doctors, dentists, veterinarians, pharmacists and midwives is now given. For the sectoral profession of nursing care, these requirements have already been implemented in the Health and Hospital Care Act. We expect that we have heard today that only a very small number of those trained abroad in the EEA meet the conditions for partial access to one of the above-mentioned health professions, so the financial impact due to the administrative needs will be very low. In all the legislative texts – and I have considered this very carefully – special attention is paid to the obligation to provide information on any partial exercise towards service providers and service providers, so that the restriction in professional practice must be recognizable to all persons concerned. There were praises from individual professional groups for the good involvement in the lawmaking process. For all who do not know: Mr Kaniak is a pharmacist and his own board has praised the implementation. I am allowed to read three sentences from the opinion of the Chamber of Pharmacy – it is, I believe, not right, that one is playing with the fears of the population and is not sure about them, Mr Colleague: “From the point of view of the Austrian Pharmacy Chamber, however, the present draft is limited to what is necessary by Union law”. We are currently not aware of a professional image across the EU which could lead to partial access to the pharmacy under this draft law. Of course, there is no rule out the fact that new job opportunities in the EU's foreign countries could be applied in the future. - Quote. In fact, it should be possible for all political groups in the House to agree to this law. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-04-27-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00153_d7e24862	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! This KFG-Novelle refers essentially to two paragraphs, and I am right to give my previous speaker, Mr Ries: There are no new sanctions on behaviour, but all that is done in this law are clarifications that facilitate the implementation of the law, which make it easier for the authority, first and foremost the road authorities, but also the criminal authorities, to make their work easier, because it is precisely what is banned. This starts with § 58. It generally regulates what the authorities are allowed to examine on-the-spot, and now gives the authorities – that is valid law – the power to disassembly the marking in the event of infringements. It is now precisely stated that these powers apply, in particular, “if it is established from its own perception that vehicle-controlled detonations, noises are generated from the final silencer by blow-like discharge of overpressure in the suction system or flames”. § 102 KFG, which is also supplemented, lays down the obligations of each vehicle driver. One of these obligations is that the vehicle must be used in a mode appropriate to the nature of the vehicle. As a result, this novelle precisely states that it is inserted, which is classified as not the nature of the vehicle's behaviour. It does not create additional criminality, but it is only specified. I think that if the media reports that have already been quoted are brought to mind, we understand the problem. There is also an excellent contribution in today’s “Falter” by Mr Matzinger, who writes very drasticly: “The gate to hell is under a Mattersburg number sign: As soon as the driver of the Audi A4 Avant Gas gives, his exhaust will be the automatic gun.” It describes this Tuning scene, which is truly perceptible – very disturbingly perceptible – in Vienna, apparently also in other federal states, and I say that it is probably justified if the legislator is already taking preventive action, because this highly growing Tuning scene actually brings with it a potential for danger, That is why it is certainly not wrong for the authorities in particular to be given the opportunity to apply the sanctions already existing in the motor vehicle law very objectively. I am someone who reads laws very critically and highly critical of the legal system. In this case, I am not willing to praise the lawyers and the legalists. The rules are very clear as regards the inclusions in the existing legislative text, as well as the additions, and should therefore seek the consent of the House in the interests of environmental protection, the protection of workers, but above all of road safety. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-04-27-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00153_d7e25703	Madam President. Minister of Art and Culture, Mr. Vice-Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen, About three weeks ago, we discussed cultural policy in the Committee about 3 hours ago, and I am sorry today that there has been no initiative from the government parties that could have been discussed in the Committee, which we could have decided or at least discussed today. According to the many reports, which are mandatory in terms of bridging aid and funds, and the annual forecast, we have then also discussed opposition requests, which have partly been shubbled, postponed, and, indeed, already common practice. We have two that have been rejected on the agenda today. I think I can already say – I do not know if you share this opinion, Minister of Art and Culture – that in the case of pandemic, not everything is done and can be considered to be abated by the large group of artists and all people working in the arts and culture business. It's not just about the pandemic being over. This is our first request, which you rejected, as if everything had already been done. The pandemic has been declared to have been terminated several times, but it has not yet been completed. In autumn, we expect – and this is what the experts, not only in Austria, but worldwide – with a next wave with a possible even more contagious variant. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, artists and all the people who work in these areas are also affected by the enormous priceyness, from the enormous priceyness that will probably not be over in the autumn. In other words, it is a question of loss of revenue, of price, of employment, which is partly not clarified. That is exactly what we wanted to discuss with you on the basis of these two requests, to discuss positively, namely that we should say: Yes, the pandemic is not over, cultural aid is also needed! without the foresighting immediately shaving over a comb and saying they are necessary for all. But, I think, it is absolutely necessary and important to get from you the commitment that these cultural aids must go on, because the pandemic of course also bears on the financial powers of the art and cultural creators. Events are still being cancelled. It is still the case that reading iron has to be postponed. In the meantime, the situation has also shifted as far as the disease is concerned: They are sick themselves and cannot carry out and use certain things. And what do you do if half of a whole festival team fails and if you don't know how the audience is, it's still observant, because in the future will also react? There are also cultural institutions that do not receive fixed basic fees, which need to handle themselves purely project-oriented and to finance themselves primarily through projects. Do they also have all the fair contracts? We also address this in our requests: What is the contractual situation like? Is there no Gage in shifts? Is there no reward in the event of cancellations? The economic risk then lies entirely individually with the artist, with the artist, and there is hardly any security or security. What is it like? with the obligation to take care of the employer, the employer? We have also put forward proposals on how to better shape the contractual situation. So we are missing a long-term strategy, Mr Federal Minister. We wanted to discuss this with you in a resolution. It wasn't possible. In the same way, in the context of the second request, in order to mention this very briefly, it was not possible to discuss with you our proposals, which we have made, so that the artist can also provide the services to the Social Security Fund, because of the fact that many artists cannot achieve these minimum incomes, because they have just had a loss of revenue, which is to suggest that we have said: Let's extend the bonus years! or other suggestions. You didn't care about that either. I believe that the artists feel rightly abandoned and remain in this uncertainty that in their precarious employment relationships – partly in their employee relationships on the other hand – in their project orientation, in which one cannot be sure whether the next project is also financed, the income limits represent a veritable problem. It would be as necessary to reform the artist's social security fund as quickly as possible. Unfortunately, this was not possible to discuss, and this is a bit of a testimonie for art and cultural policy in our country.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-04-27-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00153_d7e25853	Next speaker: Mr Volker Reifenberger. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-04-27-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00153_d7e25874	Madam President. Dear Mr. Vice-Chancellor! High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Today, we are discussing two requests from social democracy, both rejected by the government groups in the committee – one right from our point of view, but the other wrong. It should be noted, however, that it is a miracle that these two requests can be discussed at all here in the House's plenary session and that they have not been postponed in the Committee. In fact, the audience must know that long-term applications are being shoved and then do not make the way here to the National Council's plenary session. However, if the opposition's requests are rejected in the committee as here, in exceptional cases, they will be discussed at a plenary session as at least today and presented accordingly to a general public. Unfortunately, this is only very rare, and it is a violation of the Rules of Procedure for the permanent postponements by the government parties. Mr Vice-Chancellor He was himself a long enough opposition politician here in the House. But now on the content of these two applications: The first request states that the pandemic-related cultural aid is still partly necessary. - That's perfectly right. In some cases, the aid expired at the end of March: the hardness fund, the NPO fund, the bridge fund. Unfortunately, it is not the case that the cultural landscape starts from zero to a hundred and recovers from one day to another. Although fortunately many of these senseless corona measures have now been removed, the cultural area has not yet reached a situation similar to that of the pre-Corona era, and one does not yet record the full utilization that had been given before. It therefore needs a gentle transition with special financial support until normal operation is reached. Unfortunately, it is also true that cultural organizers – of course the shirt is closer than the skirt – often try to keep themselves unharmed by skillful contract design to the artists and to transfer economic risks to them, including cultural organizers who are subsidized by public means. This would require more fairness, which the State as a subsidy provider could also demand. That would also be a kind of much-quoted fair pay, so we support this request. However, we do not share the content of the second proposal from Social Democracy, as no other political group could at all be convinced of this request. From our point of view, this is clearly beyond the goal. The Künstler-Socialversicherungsfonds grants financial grants, which is good and useful, but as an artist you only get the benefit if you get a minimum amount of income from artistic activity, otherwise everyone could say: I am an artist! or it claims and thus comes to the enjoyment of such a grant. In order to ensure that this minimum income limit does not lead to inappropriate hardness cases, there are additional mechanisms, such as the five bonus years already mentioned, in which one may lower this minimum income limit without losing the grant. Furthermore, income from the self-employed artistic activity and from artistic by-productive activities as well as scholarships and prizes are counted on this minimum income limit. In short, there are many mechanisms to avoid such cases of hardness. There may nevertheless be individual cases that need to be looked at and which one must also solve, but I do not see a general structural problem, which would be legal to solve here. The proposal of social democracy is therefore overwhelming from our point of view, and it also poses the danger that this lower income may be completely removed. It would be better to evaluate how the two years of the pandemic have actually affected the artists, and to draw, on the basis of their corresponding conclusions, whether there really is a need for legislative action.

Point First reading: Request by Mr Mario Lindner, Members of Parliament, concerning a federal law amending the equal treatment law and the law on the equal treatment commission and the equal treatment attorneyship (2412/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2022-04-27-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00153_d7e26600	This brings us to the item on the agenda 12. We are going into the debate. Mr Mario Lindner, you have the floor.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-04-27-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00153_d7e26630	Highly esteemed Madam President! Mr. Vice-Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen! I quote: “What used to be sick and disgusting is now hallowable.” – Quote. This sentence does not come from any title, it does not come from a hate comment from the social media, but from the website of a Dutch hotel that proudly calls itself an “anti-homo house” and that makes it advertising that operators “reject homosexuality and want nothing to do with AIDS and syphilis” – quite legally and in the context of the laws. Ladies and gentlemen, such hateful statements are still legal in Austria today. A company may be quite open to advertising that it discriminates against gays, lesbians, bisexuals. In Austria, people are still allowed to be thrown out of places, they may be denied an apartment just because they love who they just love. The same applies to the issue of age. Discrimination due to age is also perfectly legal and legal in order in this country. In both cases, we are one of the last European countries to allow such a thing. While we remain inactive, a third of all LGBTIQ people have experienced discrimination in private life in the past 12 months, almost a quarter in locals or bars, and almost every tenth in the search for housing. We are finally in Europe when it comes to protecting our citizens from hatred, exclusion and discrimination. This is really unworthy of a country like Austria, ladies and gentlemen. [[Consistency with the SPÖ and the Abg. Grebien.]] Ladies and gentlemen, we know what this is all about, namely that a party in this House has been fighting against discrimination for more than a decade. The missing protection has a name: ÖVP. [[Abg. Hear: Hey!]] The debate on the “Anti-Homo House” has shown, however, that there is a rethinking of some colleagues from the ÖVP. It would be high time. Not only here in the National Council, but also in the Economic Chamber, ÖVP officials are now speaking out for full protection of discrimination. Let us take advantage of this moment together and finally act! We will give you the opportunity to do so with this legislative proposal. What we are starting to discuss today is no less than the most comprehensive anti-discrimination law in the last 20 years. It would finally prohibit discrimination due to sexual orientation, gender identity and age not only in private life, but also in health care, in social services or in the field of education. In short: This anti-discrimination law would finally lead Austria to the protection of human rights in the 21st century. ==References== [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, I am serious. Take advantage of this opportunity! Let us discuss it in committee! Bring in your ideas and inputs to this bill! Let us together seek the best solution for those people in our country, for whom discrimination is unfortunately still a daily one! [[Congratulations with representatives of the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen of the government groups, let us do something! Don't let party politics, political logic or fear of marginal groups in your own parties prevent a serious debate on discrimination protection! We owe it to people in Austria.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-04-27-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00153_d7e26685	Mrs Maria Smodics-Neumann is the next speaker. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-04-27-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00153_d7e26706	Madam President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen! I should also like to make a point of order. The subject of discrimination protection and the extension of the same brief position. There is already protection of discrimination in the working world, a ban on discrimination by age, sexual orientation, religion and belief in the world. Mr Lindner's request to extend this to the private sector and private contracts. In the EU, a proposal for an anti-discrimination directive has been discussed for a long time and, given the experience we have had with EU directives, we have agreed in the coalition paper to refrain from over-fulfilment of EU directives – Gold Plating. Getting Now You ask yourself the question: Why does it take so long until this directive? – Because it may not be as simple as it appears at first glance when you see the big picture behind it. I would like to ask a few questions in the room or bring a few examples. How can we ensure that, if two private individuals conclude a contract, the one’s right will not be an obligation to contravene for the other party? If we actually take the concept of equal treatment seriously, we must be very careful about special rights for individual groups. What's it look like? How do we guarantee the fundamental right of contract and property rights? How can we keep that? How do we deal with a turn of evidence? What does that mean? – One Party feels discriminated against, demonstrates this discrimination to the Equality Commission, and the other Party must prove that it has not discriminated against it. Will that really be possible in all cases? How do we continue to ensure that there are products from the economy that we love to gain? Think of seniors, children's hotels, the "Woman" day or the Vienna line ticket for LGTIQ visitors, surely a heart project also of social democracy! Of course, what is true of one in one direction must also apply in the other direction. This is really just a very, very short and incomplete demolition of the problem of levelling-up. The respected lawyer Prof. Tomandl was quoted in the press, and I would like to do so here. He says: The levelling-up of discrimination protection is “the most massive intervention into the freedom you can imagine”. The allocation will allow us to deal with these issues in the Committee. Thank you.

It is not about it that in autokratia human rights are first to be circumcised that human rights organisations have been banned in Russia as the first human rights organisations. Thank you.
